199	                    provider_name, len(all_candidate_windows))
200	
201	        def process_candidate_window(wi: int, window: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[dict]:
202	            w_start = max(0, window["start"] - handoff_padding)
203	            w_end = min(video_duration, window["end"] + handoff_padding)
204	            w_dur = w_end - w_start
205	            w_path = os.path.join(handoff_dir, f"handoff_{video_id}_{wi}.mp4")
206	            if not extract_video_chunk(video_path, w_start, w_dur, w_path, reencode=True):
207	                return []
208	            prompt = sport_profile.get_prompt(chunk_duration=w_dur)
209	            segments, _ = provider.analyze_video(w_path, prompt, chunk_duration=w_dur,
210	                                                 label=f"Handoff {wi+1}")
211	            valid = []
212	            for seg in segments:
213	                try:
214	                    seg["start_time"] = float(seg["start_time"]) + w_start
215	                    seg["end_time"] = float(seg["end_time"]) + w_start
216	                    seg["_candidate_id"] = candidate_ids[wi]
217	                    valid.append(seg)
218	                except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as e:
219	                    logger.warning("Dropping segment with unparseable timestamps: %s — %s", seg, e)
220	            try:
221	                os.remove(w_path)
222	            except OSError:
223	                pass
224	            return valid
225	
226	        ai_segments: List[dict] = []
227	        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=ai_max_workers) as executor:
228	            futures = [executor.submit(process_candidate_window, wi, cw)
229	                       for wi, cw in enumerate(all_candidate_windows)]
230	            for future in futures:
231	                ai_segments.extend(future.result())
232	        try:
233	            os.rmdir(handoff_dir)
234	        except OSError:
235	            pass
236	        logger.info("Phase 3 completed. AI confirmed %d semantic play segments.", len(ai_segments))
237	
238	        # --- Phase 4: DB population + candidate linking ---
239	        segments_data = []
240	        for segment in ai_segments:
241	            try:
242	                start = float(segment["start_time"])
243	                end = float(segment["end_time"])
244	                dur = end - start
245	                metadata = {
246	                    "shot_count": self._shot_count_from(segment, dur),
247	                    "shot_count_confidence": segment.get("shot_count_confidence", "Unknown"),
248	                    "detector": f"{self.family}-hybrid-{model_name}",
249	                }
250	                segment_id = self.db.add_play_segment(video_id, start, end, None, None, metadata)
251	                if segment_id:
252	                    candidate_id = segment.get("_candidate_id")
253	                    if candidate_id is not None:
254	                        self.db.link_candidate_to_segment(candidate_id, segment_id)
255	                    segments_data.append({
256	                        "id": segment_id, "start_time": start, "end_time": end,
257	                        "duration": dur, "metadata": metadata,
258	                    })
259	            except Exception as e:
260	                logger.warning("Skipping invalid segment struct: %s", e)
261	
262	        return segments_data

The root cause is clear from the code. `textfile=` uses a path under `tmp_dir` (relative `output/tmp…` → no drive letter), which is why text never errored. But `_BUNDLED_FONT` (added in PR #103) is an **absolute** path `C:\…\RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf` → `_ff_quote` turns it into `C:/…` and the **drive-letter colon breaks ffmpeg's drawtext option parsing** (`:` separates filter options). Let me confirm `_BUNDLED_FONT` and reproduce/verify the fix empirically before touching code:

Confirmed: `_BUNDLED_FONT` is absolute (`C:\…`) and exists. Now let me reproduce the failure and verify the fix empirically — mirroring exactly how the compiler invokes ffmpeg (subprocess list, no shell), testing the raw `C:/` path vs. a colon-escaped one:

### 325. assistant (2026-06-13T09:35:52.646Z)

"""Reproduce the watermark drawtext failure + verify the colon-escape fix.

Mirrors VideoCompiler exactly: ffmpeg invoked via subprocess list (no shell),
so only ffmpeg filter-level escaping matters, not shell escaping.
"""
import subprocess
import tempfile
import os

FONT = r"C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer\backend\assets\fonts\RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf"


def ff_quote_current(path: str) -> str:
    # current VideoCompiler._ff_quote
    return path.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'")


def ff_quote_fixed(path: str) -> str:
    # proposed: also escape the drive-letter (and any) colon for ffmpeg filter parsing
    return path.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'").replace(":", r"\:")


def try_filter(label: str, font_quoted: str) -> None:
    out = os.path.join(tempfile.gettempdir(), f"wm_{label}.mp4")
    vf = (f"drawtext=text='hi':fontfile='{font_quoted}'"
          f":fontcolor=white@0.85:fontsize=20:x=10:y=10:box=1:boxcolor=black@0.50:boxborderw=10")
    cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-f", "lavfi", "-i", "testsrc=size=320x240:duration=1:rate=10",
           "-vf", vf, "-frames:v", "3", out]
    r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True)
    ok = r.returncode == 0 and os.path.exists(out) and os.path.getsize(out) > 0
    print(f"[{label}] vf={vf}")
    print(f"[{label}] returncode={r.returncode} ok={ok}")
    if not ok:
        print(f"[{label}] stderr tail: {r.stderr.decode(errors='replace')[-300:]}")
    print()


print("CURRENT _ff_quote (raw C:/ colon):")
try_filter("current", ff_quote_current(FONT))
print("FIXED _ff_quote (escaped C\\:/ colon):")

### 330. assistant (2026-06-13T09:36:49.313Z)

"""Verify the colon-escape fix is also correct for the logo `movie=` filter source."""
import subprocess
import tempfile
import os

LOGO = os.path.join(tempfile.gettempdir(), "wm_logo.png")  # created beforehand


def ff_quote_current(p):
    return p.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'")


def ff_quote_fixed(p):
    return p.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'").replace(":", r"\:")


def try_movie(label, logo_quoted):
    out = os.path.join(tempfile.gettempdir(), f"mv_{label}.mp4")
    vf = f"movie='{logo_quoted}'[wm];[in][wm]overlay=W-w-24:H-h-24"
    cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-f", "lavfi", "-i", "testsrc=size=320x240:duration=1:rate=10",
           "-vf", vf, "-frames:v", "3", out]
    r = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True)
    ok = r.returncode == 0 and os.path.exists(out) and os.path.getsize(out) > 0
    print(f"[{label}] ok={ok} rc={r.returncode}")
    if not ok:
        print(f"[{label}] stderr tail: {r.stderr.decode(errors='replace')[-200:]}")


print("movie= CURRENT (raw colon):")
try_movie("current", ff_quote_current(LOGO))
print("movie= FIXED (escaped colon):")
try_movie("fixed", ff_quote_fixed(LOGO))

### 352. assistant (2026-06-13T09:42:25.459Z)

---
name: gotcha-long-runs-gpu-wsl
description: "Running the WASB GPU pipeline E2E on a big 4K video: Modern Standby kills long runs; cv2 frame extraction is the bottleneck; the proxy+keepawake+ffmpeg-preextract recipe."
metadata:
  type: project
---

Hard-won recipe for running the indexer E2E on the GPU over a LONG/large video (e.g. the
12.8-min 4K60 GoPro `GX010125.MP4`, 11.5 GB). First proven 2026-06-13.

**Three traps, in the order they bite:**

1. **Modern Standby (connected standby) silently kills long runs.** This box enters S0
   low-power when idle/screen-off and the Desktop Activity Moderator SUSPENDS Win32 processes +
   pauses the WSL2 VM — the Python orchestrator and WSL inference freeze with NO clean exit, no
   timeout logged (the log just stops). `powercfg standby-timeout=0` does NOT prevent it (it's
   screen-off-triggered, not idle-timeout). Confirm via `Get-WinEvent ... Kernel-Power` session
   transitions. **Fix:** hold a wake lock for the whole run — a background PowerShell loop calling
   `SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS|ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED|ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED)` and staying
   alive (the flag releases when the thread dies). Saved as `scratch/keep_awake.ps1`. This is the
   same class as the older "idle/suspend kills background runs" note in [[current-state]].

2. **cv2 PNG frame extraction is the real bottleneck, NOT the GPU.** `wasb_infer.extract_frames`
   writes 46k PNGs single-threaded (~20 fps → ~38 min for a 12.8-min clip) BEFORE any GPU work.
   The GPU detector pass is fast (~25 win/s, ~26 min for 46k windows) and is checkpointed/resumable
   (`<out>_wasbcache/{det_raw.jsonl,manifest.json}`, fsync every 1000 windows). **Fix:** pre-extract
   with ffmpeg natively inside WSL (ext4, not /mnt) to PNG `%08d.png -start_number 0`; the count is
   provably exact for CFR (duration×fps = 46080) so `extract_frames` sees `existing>=expected` and
   skips its slow path entirely. wasb_infer's docstring literally recommends this but the runner
   never wired a `--frames_dir` passthrough.

3. **4K is decode-bound for WASB.** Downscale to a 1080p PROXY first (GPU `h264_nvenc`, ~4.7×
   realtime), index the proxy, but STITCH the final reel from the 4K ORIGINAL — timestamps transfer
   1:1 (identical timeline). The runner I used: `scratch/run_gx010125.py` (INDEX_PATH=proxy,
   SOURCE_PATH=4K). 1080p is also the resolution all golden evals used.

**Observability while it runs** (the user explicitly hates silent gaps): WASB inference is a WSL
subprocess so its logs are captured, NOT in the live run log — poll the cache `manifest.json`
windows_done/windows_total instead. Phase-3 Gemini progress: watch `output/chunks_gemini_handoff/`
(one `handoff_<id>_<wi>.mp4` per candidate, deleted after). DB segments only land in Phase 4 (after
ALL Gemini calls), so the DB shows no incremental Phase-3 progress.

**Result of the proven run:** 64.8% shuttle visibility, 18 candidate windows → 52 Gemini-confirmed
rallies → 11 with shot_count>7 → 2:23 4K reel. Preflight Gemini with a free `models.list` before
the long GPU pass so a dead key doesn't waste 30 min.

## Two things fixed along the way (both merged on green CI, per your standing authorization)

1. **[#107](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/107)** — API `min_shot_count` parity (above).
2. **[#108](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/108)** — a real bug the run surfaced: the on-by-default watermark (bundled font from #103) was **silently failing on Windows** — the absolute font path's drive-letter colon (`C:/…`) broke ffmpeg's drawtext parser, so every clip fell back to no-watermark. Fixed by escaping the colon in `_ff_quote` (also repaired absolute logo paths). Verified against real ffmpeg, with regression tests.

## Why the earlier attempts died (not a code bug)

**Modern Standby** suspended the machine overnight twice, freezing the run mid-inference. Fixed with a keep-awake lock. I also found the actual bottleneck was **cv2 frame extraction** (~38 min, where it kept dying — *before* the GPU even started), so I pre-extract with ffmpeg now (count-exact for CFR) and the full run completed in one ~47-min pass. The whole recipe is saved to memory for next time.

## Two notes

- **`config.json` has local uncommitted changes** from this run (`default_segmenter=wasb_hybrid`, `min_shot_count=8`, `wasb.timeout_sec=7200`). Tell me if you want them reverted to the defaults (gemini / 1 / 1800).
- **The delivered reel has no watermark** (it stitched *before* #108 landed). If you'd like a branded version, I can re-stitch — the 52 rallies are cached in the DB, so it skips all inference/Gemini and just re-cuts the 11 clips (~5 min) now that the watermark works. Want me to?

"""Re-stitch ONLY (no re-inference): reuse the 52 cached Gemini-confirmed rallies
for GX010125 and build a new reel with shot_count >= 5, cut from the 4K original.

Pulls segments straight from the DB by the known video_id, so it does not touch
WASB / Gemini / the proxy at all — pure reuse of the cached info.
"""
import json
import logging
import os
import sys
import time

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()
logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO,
                    format="%(asctime)s [%(name)s] %(levelname)s: %(message)s", datefmt="%H:%M:%S")

from backend.infrastructure.database import Database
from backend.config import load_config
from backend.pipeline.stitcher.compiler import VideoCompiler

VIDEO_ID = "89605e80ed012ca259a640b7ec06cd80"            # the 1080p-proxy MD5 we indexed
SOURCE_PATH = r"F:\GoPro Hero Black 12\KhelSutra\Badminton 12 June 2026\GX010125.MP4"  # 4K original
OUTPUT_FILENAME = "GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4"
MIN_SHOTS = 5                                            # user request: shot_count >= 5


def main():
    cfg = load_config()
    db = Database(os.path.join(cfg.output.output_dir, cfg.output.db_name))
    segs = db.query_play_segments(VIDEO_ID, {})
    print(f"[REUSE] {len(segs)} cached rallies for video_id={VIDEO_ID}", flush=True)
    if not segs:
        raise SystemExit("no cached segments — nothing to reuse")
    if not os.path.exists(SOURCE_PATH):
        raise SystemExit(f"4K source not found: {SOURCE_PATH}")

    t0 = time.time()
    time_pairs, kept, dropped = [], 0, 0
    for s in sorted(segs, key=lambda x: x["start_time"]):
        meta = s.get("metadata") or {}
        if isinstance(meta, str):
            try:
                meta = json.loads(meta)
            except Exception:
                meta = {}
        sc = meta.get("shot_count", 0)
        tag = f"  [{float(s['start_time']):8.2f}s - {float(s['end_time']):8.2f}s] shot_count={sc}"
        if sc >= MIN_SHOTS:
            time_pairs.append((s["start_time"], s["end_time"]))
            kept += 1
            print(tag + "  -> KEEP", flush=True)
        else:
            dropped += 1
            print(tag + "  -> drop", flush=True)
    print(f"[FILTER] shot_count>={MIN_SHOTS}: kept {kept}, dropped {dropped}", flush=True)
    if not time_pairs:
        raise SystemExit("no rallies passed the filter")

    compiler = VideoCompiler(cfg.output.output_dir,
                             begin_padding=cfg.stitching.begin_padding,
                             end_padding=cfg.stitching.end_padding,
                             watermark=cfg.stitching.watermark)
    out = compiler.compile_highlights(SOURCE_PATH, time_pairs, OUTPUT_FILENAME)  # cut from 4K
    print(f"[DONE] {os.path.abspath(out)} ({kept} rallies, {time.time()-t0:.0f}s)", flush=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 378. assistant (2026-06-13T14:38:37.089Z)

Both your questions answered, the flowchart is above, and the ≥5 reel is now building (reusing cache — no WASB/Gemini re-run).

## 1. Resolution: Gemini saw **1080p**, not 4K

The pipeline deliberately splits resolution by stage:
- **WASB + Gemini operated on the 1080p proxy.** The candidate clips uploaded to Gemini were `extract_video_chunk(<1080p proxy>, …, reencode=True)` with no further downscaling — so 1080p reached Gemini. This is the right trade: shot-counting and boundary detection don't need 4K, and it keeps WASB decode and Gemini upload fast/cheap.
- **The 4K original was used only for the final stitch** — that's why your reel came out true 3840×2160 while the analysis ran on 1080p.

## 2. Flowchart — where WASB sits, how "rally end" is decided

The diagram above traces it, but the key idea is **rally boundaries are decided in two passes** (the two purple/pink-flagged nodes):

- **WASB (coarse, motion-based)** — runs on the GPU over the 1080p frames and produces a per-frame shuttle trajectory `(x, y, visible)`. `trajectory_to_action_windows` turns that into candidate rally windows: **shuttle moving = in-rally**; short pauses are fused (`dead_time_merge_gap`), and a long stretch of stillness/invisibility ends the window. That's the *approximate* "where could a rally be" — it found 18 windows.
- **Gemini (fine, semantic)** — each candidate window is cut to a padded 1080p clip and sent to Gemini, which watches the actual play and returns the **exact** rally start/end (start = racket-to-shuttle contact, end = grounding/fault) plus the `shot_count`. One WASB window can contain several real rallies — that's how 18 windows became 52 confirmed rallies.

So: **WASB proposes, Gemini disposes.** The shot count that your filter uses is Gemini's `shuttle_exchange_count`.

## 3. The ≥5 reel is building now

`output\GX010125_min5_stitch.log` is the live log. It's reusing the **24 cached rallies with shot_count ≥ 5** (vs 11 at >7) straight from the DB — **no WASB or Gemini re-run** — and cutting from the 4K original. Output will be `output\GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4`. ETA ~8–12 min (24 clips re-encoded at 4K).

One note: this reel **will have the watermark** ("Generated by Khelsutra.guru"), because the bundled-font fix (#108) is now in place and the config default is on — so it also doubles as live confirmation that fix works on real 4K. Say the word if you'd prefer it without and I'll regenerate watermark-free in a couple minutes. I'll confirm the count, duration, and 4K resolution when it lands.

1	"""TrackNet shuttle-trajectory runner (WSL2 subprocess adapter).
2	
3	This bridges the Windows-side indexer to a TensorFlow TrackNetV4 install living
4	inside WSL2 (ADR-001 in docs/DECISIONS.md). The flow is:
5	
6	    1. Translate the Windows video path to its WSL-visible form (and optionally
7	       stage it into the WSL filesystem to avoid slow /mnt/c per-frame reads).
8	    2. Invoke ``src/predict.py`` inside the TrackNetV4 conda env via ``wsl``.
9	    3. Read back the per-frame ``Frame,Visibility,X,Y`` CSV it writes.
10	    4. Convert that trajectory into high-motion "action windows" — the same
11	       candidate-rally shape HybridYoloSegmenter produces — so the rest of the
12	       pipeline (AI handoff, DB population) is unchanged.
13	
14	Nothing here imports TensorFlow; all model code stays in WSL.
15	"""
16	
17	import os
18	import csv
19	import shlex
20	import logging
21	import subprocess
22	from dataclasses import dataclass
23	from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional, Tuple
24	
25	from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import DetectorRunner, WslCommandMixin, wsl_tilde_quote
26	
27	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
28	
29	
30	@dataclass
31	class TrackNetConfig:
32	    """Resolved settings for a TrackNet WSL run (built from config.json -> indexing.tracknet)."""
33	    distro: str = "Ubuntu"
34	    repo_dir: str = "~/models/TrackNetV4"          # WSL path to the cloned repo
35	    conda_sh: str = "~/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh"
36	    conda_env: str = "TrackNetV4"
37	    weights_path: str = ""                          # WSL path to the .h5/.keras weights (REQUIRED)
38	    queue_length: int = 5
39	    stage_in_wsl: bool = True                       # copy video into WSL fs first (perf)
40	    wsl_stage_dir: str = "~/clips"                  # where staged videos/outputs live in WSL
41	    timeout_sec: int = 1800                         # 30 min; kills a hung call (0 = no timeout)
42	
43	    @classmethod
44	    def from_indexing_cfg(cls, idx_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> "TrackNetConfig":
45	        tn = (idx_cfg or {}).get("tracknet", {}) or {}
46	        return cls(
47	            distro=tn.get("wsl_distro", "Ubuntu"),
48	            repo_dir=tn.get("repo_dir", "~/models/TrackNetV4"),
49	            conda_sh=tn.get("conda_sh", "~/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh"),
50	            conda_env=tn.get("conda_env", "TrackNetV4"),
51	            weights_path=tn.get("weights_path", ""),
52	            queue_length=int(tn.get("queue_length", 5)),
53	            stage_in_wsl=bool(tn.get("stage_in_wsl", True)),
54	            wsl_stage_dir=tn.get("wsl_stage_dir", "~/clips"),
55	            timeout_sec=int(tn.get("timeout_sec", 1800)),
56	        )
57	
58	
59	def to_wsl_mnt_path(win_path: str) -> str:
60	    """Translate a Windows path (C:\\Users\\x) to its WSL /mnt form (/mnt/c/Users/x).
61	
62	    Already-POSIX paths (starting with / or ~) are returned unchanged so the
63	    same helper is safe to call on either kind of path.
64	    """
65	    p = str(win_path)
66	    if p.startswith("/") or p.startswith("~"):
67	        return p
68	    p = p.replace("\\", "/")
69	    if len(p) >= 2 and p[1] == ":":
70	        drive = p[0].lower()
71	        return f"/mnt/{drive}{p[2:]}"
72	    return p
73	
74	
75	@dataclass
76	class TrajectoryPoint:
77	    frame: int
78	    visible: bool
79	    x: float
80	    y: float
81	
82	
83	class TrackNetRunner(WslCommandMixin, DetectorRunner):
84	    """Runs TrackNet predict.py in WSL and turns the output CSV into action windows.
85	
86	    Implements the common DetectorRunner contract so a future Linux-native or
87	    alternative trajectory runner can be substituted without touching the hybrids.
88	    """
89	
90	    def __init__(self, cfg: TrackNetConfig):
91	        self.cfg = cfg
92	
93	    def healthcheck(self) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
94	        """Verify the WSL env is usable: conda env exists, TF imports, GPU visible.
95	
96	        Returns (ok, message). Cheap to call before a long run.
97	        """
98	        cmd = (
99	            f"conda activate {self.cfg.conda_env} && "
100	            f"python -c \"import tensorflow as tf; "
101	            f"print('TF', tf.__version__, 'GPUs', len(tf.config.list_physical_devices('GPU')))\""
102	        )
103	        try:
104	            res = self._wsl_bash(cmd)
105	        except FileNotFoundError:
106	            return False, "`wsl` not found — is this running on Windows with WSL2 installed?"
107	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
108	            return False, "WSL healthcheck timed out."
109	        out = (res.stdout or "").strip()
110	        if res.returncode != 0:
111	            return False, f"WSL env check failed: {(res.stderr or out).strip()}"
112	        return True, out
113	
114	    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- inference
115	
116	    def run_predict(self, video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str,
117	                    video_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:
118	        """Run predict.py on a video. Returns the Windows path to the produced CSV, or None.
119	
120	        ``output_win_dir`` is a Windows directory (under the repo's output/) that
121	        WSL writes into via its /mnt view, so the Windows process can read the CSV
122	        back with no copy. ``video_id`` (file MD5) namespaces the staged video — and
123	        therefore predict.py's ``<stem>_predict.csv`` output — so two videos that share
124	        a filename cannot collide on the output CSV.
125	        """
126	        if not self.cfg.weights_path:
127	            logger.error(
128	                "TrackNet weights_path is not set. Set indexing.tracknet.weights_path "
129	                "in config.json to the WSL path of your trained TrackNetV4 weights."
130	            )
131	            return None
132	
133	        # Resolve relative dirs BEFORE the /mnt translation — to_wsl_mnt_path passes
134	        # relative paths through unchanged, so WSL would resolve them against the
135	        # predict.py cwd instead of the repo (same bug class as wasb_runner).
136	        output_win_dir = os.path.abspath(output_win_dir)
137	        os.makedirs(output_win_dir, exist_ok=True)
138	        # Normalize separators so basename/splitext work when tests (or collector)
139	        # pass Windows-style paths on a Linux host.
140	        video_win_path = str(video_win_path).replace("\\", "/")
141	        base = os.path.basename(video_win_path)
142	        stem, ext = os.path.splitext(base)
143	
144	        # predict.py only accepts .avi/.mp4 and names the CSV "<stem>_predict.csv".
145	        if ext.lower() not in (".mp4", ".avi"):
146	            logger.error("TrackNet predict.py only supports .mp4/.avi (got %s)", ext)
147	            return None
148	
149	        wsl_out = to_wsl_mnt_path(output_win_dir)
150	        # Namespace by video_id so two same-filename videos don't collide on the CSV.
151	        # predict.py derives its output name from the (staged) video stem, so staging
152	        # under the namespaced name propagates into "<key>_predict.csv".
153	        key = f"{stem}__{video_id[:12]}" if video_id else stem
154	        expected_csv_win = os.path.join(output_win_dir, f"{key}_predict.csv")
155	
156	        # Resolve the video path WSL will read.
157	        if self.cfg.stage_in_wsl:
158	            staged = self._stage_video(video_win_path, f"{key}{ext}")
159	            if staged is None:
160	                return None
161	            wsl_video = staged
162	        else:
163	            # No staging: predict.py reads /mnt directly and writes "<stem>_predict.csv".
164	            # We cannot rename its output, so fall back to the un-namespaced name.
165	            wsl_video = to_wsl_mnt_path(video_win_path)
166	            expected_csv_win = os.path.join(output_win_dir, f"{stem}_predict.csv")
167	
168	        cmd = (
169	            f"conda activate {self.cfg.conda_env} && "
170	            f"cd {self.cfg.repo_dir}/src && "
171	            f"python predict.py "
172	            f"--video_path {wsl_tilde_quote(wsl_video)} "
173	            f"--model_weights {wsl_tilde_quote(self.cfg.weights_path)} "
174	            f"--output_dir {wsl_tilde_quote(wsl_out)} "
175	            f"--queue_length {self.cfg.queue_length}"
176	        )
177	        logger.info("Running TrackNet inference on %s ...", base)
178	        try:
179	            res = self._wsl_bash(cmd)
180	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
181	            logger.error("TrackNet inference timed out after %ss.", self.cfg.timeout_sec)
182	            return None
183	
184	        if res.returncode != 0:
185	            logger.error("TrackNet predict.py failed:\n%s", (res.stderr or res.stdout)[-2000:])
186	            return None
187	
188	        if not os.path.exists(expected_csv_win):
189	            logger.error("TrackNet finished but expected CSV not found at %s", expected_csv_win)
190	            return None
191	        logger.info("TrackNet trajectory CSV: %s", expected_csv_win)
192	        return expected_csv_win
193	
194	    def _stage_video(self, video_win_path: str, base: str) -> Optional[str]:
195	        """Copy the input video into the WSL filesystem (avoids slow /mnt/c reads).
196	
197	        Returns the WSL path to the staged copy, or None on failure.
198	        """
199	        src_mnt = to_wsl_mnt_path(video_win_path)
200	        dest = f"{self.cfg.wsl_stage_dir}/{base}"
201	        cmd = f"mkdir -p {self.cfg.wsl_stage_dir} && cp {shlex.quote(src_mnt)} {wsl_tilde_quote(dest)} && echo OK"
202	        try:
203	            res = self._wsl_bash(cmd)
204	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
205	            logger.error("Staging video into WSL timed out.")
206	            return None
207	        if res.returncode != 0 or "OK" not in (res.stdout or ""):
208	            logger.error("Failed to stage video into WSL: %s", (res.stderr or res.stdout).strip())
209	            return None
210	        return dest
211	
212	    # -------------------------------------------------------------- CSV -> windows
213	
214	    @staticmethod
215	    def parse_trajectory_csv(csv_win_path: str) -> List[TrajectoryPoint]:
216	        """Parse a TrackNet predict CSV (columns: Frame,Visibility,X,Y)."""
217	        points: List[TrajectoryPoint] = []
218	        with open(csv_win_path, newline="") as f:
219	            reader = csv.DictReader(f)
220	            for row in reader:
221	                try:
222	                    points.append(TrajectoryPoint(
223	                        frame=int(row["Frame"]),
224	                        visible=int(row["Visibility"]) == 1,
225	                        x=float(row["X"]),
226	                        y=float(row["Y"]),
227	                    ))
228	                except (KeyError, ValueError):
229	                    continue
230	        points.sort(key=lambda p: p.frame)
231	        return points
232	
233	    @staticmethod
234	    def trajectory_to_action_windows(
235	        points: List[TrajectoryPoint],
236	        fps: float,
237	        frame_width: float,
238	        chunk_start: float = 0.0,
239	        velocity_thresh: float = 0.02,
240	        merge_gap: float = 2.0,
241	        min_window_duration: float = 2.0,
242	        chunk_index: Optional[int] = None,
243	    ) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
244	        """Convert a shuttle trajectory into candidate rally windows.
245	
246	        A frame is "active" when the shuttle is visible AND its inter-frame
247	        displacement (normalised by frame width, per second) exceeds
248	        ``velocity_thresh`` — i.e. the shuttle is in flight. Active frames within
249	        ``merge_gap`` seconds of each other are grouped into one window; short
250	        windows are dropped. Mirrors HybridYoloSegmenter's windowing so the dict
251	        shape feeding the AI-handoff phase is identical.
252	        """
253	        if fps <= 0:
254	            fps = 30.0
255	        if frame_width <= 0:
256	            frame_width = 1280.0
257	
258	        # Compute per-frame normalised velocity from the last *visible* point.
259	        active = []  # (frame_idx, velocity)
260	        prev: Optional[TrajectoryPoint] = None
261	        for p in points:
262	            if not p.visible:
263	                prev = None  # break the trajectory; absent shuttle = not in flight
264	                continue
265	            if prev is not None:
266	                dframes = p.frame - prev.frame
267	                if dframes > 0:
268	                    dist = ((p.x - prev.x) ** 2 + (p.y - prev.y) ** 2) ** 0.5
269	                    velocity = (dist / frame_width) * (fps / dframes)
270	                    if velocity > velocity_thresh:
271	                        active.append((p.frame, velocity))
272	            prev = p
273	
274	        if not active:
275	            return []
276	
277	        max_gap_frames = int(merge_gap * fps)
278	
279	        def _finalize(group: List[Tuple[int, float]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
280	            vels = [v for _, v in group]
281	            return {
282	                "start": group[0][0] / fps + chunk_start,
283	                "end": group[-1][0] / fps + chunk_start,
284	                "active_frame_count": len(group),
285	                "peak_velocity": max(vels),
286	                "mean_velocity": sum(vels) / len(vels),
287	                "track_id_count": 1,  # single shuttle; kept for window-shape parity
288	                "chunk_index": chunk_index,
289	            }
290	
291	        windows = []
292	        group = [active[0]]
293	        for af in active[1:]:
294	            if af[0] - group[-1][0] <= max_gap_frames:
295	                group.append(af)
296	            else:
297	                windows.append(_finalize(group))
298	                group = [af]
299	        windows.append(_finalize(group))
300	
301	        return [w for w in windows if (w["end"] - w["start"]) >= min_window_duration]
302

### 385. assistant (2026-06-13T14:46:13.528Z)

Now I can give you the full picture with exact pointers. Pinning links to the current commit (`6de70aa`) so the line numbers stay stable.

## Where `trajectory_to_action_windows` comes from

It's **the indexer's own code** — not from WASB or TrackNet upstream. It was written as part of the **TrackNet WSL adapter** (ADR-001), and is deliberately **model-agnostic**: it lives as a `@staticmethod` on `TrackNetRunner`, and `WasbRunner` reuses it verbatim because both detectors emit the **same `Frame,Visibility,X,Y` trajectory CSV**:

- [`tracknet_runner.py::trajectory_to_action_windows`](backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py:233) — the windowing logic → [GitHub L233–L301](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py#L233-L301)
- [`tracknet_runner.py::parse_trajectory_csv`](backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py:214) — CSV → points → [GitHub L214–L231](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py#L214-L231)
- [`wasb_runner.py`](backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py:154) — WASB inheriting both, the "model-agnostic" seam → [GitHub L154–L156](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py#L154-L156)

## How the *trajectory* itself was detected (the `(x, y, visible)` per frame)

That's WASB, wrapped in [`wasb_infer.py::run`](backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py:207) ([GitHub](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L207-L345)). Two upstream building blocks from **[nttcom/WASB-SBDT](https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT)** (MIT; "Widely Applicable Strong Baseline for Sports Ball Detection & Tracking", Tarashima et al., BMVC 2023):

1. **Detector** (`build_detector` → `detector.run_tensor`, [L297](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L297)): a heatmap CNN (HRNet-style backbone). It takes a **short stack of consecutive frames** (`frames_in`, here 3 — that's why 46,080 frames → 46,078 windows) so it can use motion, and predicts a Gaussian **heatmap** per frame. The peak of the heatmap = the shuttle's pixel location; peak confidence below threshold ⇒ "not visible" that frame.
2. **Online tracker** (`build_tracker` → `tracker.update`, [L338](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L338)): links the per-frame peaks into one coherent track (position/motion association), rejecting spurious blobs and filling `Visibility=0, X=Y=-1` where the shuttle is genuinely lost.

The result is one row per frame — `Frame, Visibility(0/1), X, Y` — which is exactly what `parse_trajectory_csv` reads. (Your run: 46,080 rows, 29,879 visible = 64.8%.)

## How `trajectory_to_action_windows` turns that into candidate rallies

The core is a **velocity gate + temporal grouping**:

```python
# per consecutive VISIBLE pair: speed as fraction-of-frame-width per second
dist     = ((p.x - prev.x)**2 + (p.y - prev.y)**2) ** 0.5
velocity = (dist / frame_width) * (fps / dframes)
if velocity > velocity_thresh:        # shuttle is "in flight"
    active.append((p.frame, velocity))
# an INVISIBLE frame resets prev → absent shuttle = not in flight (a natural break)
```
```python
# group active frames; a gap longer than merge_gap closes the window
max_gap_frames = int(merge_gap * fps)
if af.frame - group[-1].frame <= max_gap_frames:
    group.append(af)        # same rally — short pause tolerated
else:
    windows.append(_finalize(group)); group = [af]   # new rally
...
return [w for w in windows if (w.end - w.start) >= min_window_duration]
```

Step by step:
1. **Velocity** is normalized by frame width (so it's resolution-independent — works identically on 1080p) and converted to per-second. Above `velocity_thresh` ⇒ the frame is "active" (shuttle flying).
2. **An invisible shuttle breaks the trajectory** (`prev = None`) — disappearance is itself a boundary signal.
3. **Grouping** merges active frames within `merge_gap` seconds into one window — so the brief pauses *between shots within a rally* don't split it, but a long stillness (shuttle down, players resetting) **ends** the window. ← this is the "coarse rally boundary" from the flowchart.
4. Windows shorter than `min_window_duration` are dropped (kills jitter/false blips). Each window carries `start/end`, `peak_velocity`, `mean_velocity`, `active_frame_count`.

These three knobs come from `config.json → indexing.wasb`:
- `velocity_threshold` (0.02) → `velocity_thresh`
- `dead_time_merge_gap` (2.0s) → `merge_gap`
- `min_action_window_duration` (2.0s) → `min_window_duration`

So WASB gives you *motion-derived* candidate windows (18 of them on your video); Gemini then refines each into precise rallies with shot counts. The windowing is intentionally the same shape `HybridYoloSegmenter` produces, which is why the AI-handoff + DB stages are detector-agnostic.

## Your GPU right now: idle of pipeline work

`32% util, 2.2 GB, 21 W` — that's just **desktop apps** holding the GPU for compositing/decode (Edge WebView, explorer, WhatsApp, the Claude app, VS Code, and **VLC** — possibly you previewing the reel). **No compute job is running** — no `python` or `ffmpeg` process, and no ML/encode load (the 2070 Super pulls ~80 W under real load; 21 W is idle). The ≥5 stitch already finished.

## Does stitching use the GPU? **No — it's CPU.**

The compiler encodes with the **software x264 encoder**, not NVENC:
- Per-clip re-encode: [`-c:v libx264 -preset superfast`](backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py:248) (CPU)
- Final join: [`-c copy`](backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py:332) (stream-copy, no encoding at all)

So the ~11-minute ≥5 stitch was **CPU-bound** (24 clips of 4K x264 + a watermark drawtext filter, which is also a CPU filter). That's why GPU stayed low while it ran.

**For contrast, what *did* run on the GPU earlier:**
- The **4K→1080p proxy transcode** — `h264_nvenc` (GPU encoder).
- **WASB shuttle inference** — the detector CNN on CUDA.

So in this whole job, the GPU did the *analysis* (WASB) and the *proxy*, while the *final stitch* is CPU.

## ≥5 reel is done and verified: `output\GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4`

- **24 rallies** (shot_count ≥ 5), **true 4K** 3840×2160, 59.94 fps, **4:08**, 1987 MB, cut from the original.
- **0 watermark failures** this time (vs 12/12 on the >7 reel) — so the watermark is now correctly burned in. That's a clean end-to-end confirmation that the #108 fix works on real 4K footage.

You now have both: `GX010125_highlights.mp4` (11 rallies, >7 shots, no watermark) and `GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4` (24 rallies, ≥5 shots, watermarked).

---

If the 11-minute CPU stitch felt slow: I can make the stitcher **GPU-encode with NVENC** (decode `-hwaccel cuda` + `-c:v h264_nvenc`), which would cut 4K stitching from ~minutes to ~tens of seconds. It's a legit improvement, but it needs a **software fallback** (NVENC isn't present on the Linux CI or non-NVIDIA machines) and a CRF→CQ quality mapping, so I'd do it as a proper PR with the fallback rather than hard-switching. Want me to take that on?

### 419. assistant (2026-06-13T15:11:50.053Z)

Here's a plan for keeping cache hygiene healthy without it becoming a chore — built around what we actually saw accumulate.

## The core problem

The bloat is **regenerable intermediates**, dominated by one thing: PNG frame extraction materializes every frame to disk — **93 GB for a single 12.8-min 4K video**. Across many videos that's unbounded. Staged video copies (2 GB+ each) pile on. Meanwhile the *valuable* outputs are tiny or already archived. So the principle is simple:

> **Separate regenerable intermediates (purge aggressively, automatically) from durable outputs (keep/archive). Make purging default-safe — dry-run first, the "show me before you delete" pattern.**

## Retention policy — what lives where, and its lifespan

| Artifact | Location | Regenerable? | Policy |
|---|---|---|---|
| `*_frames/` (PNG decode) | WSL `~/clips` | ✅ from video | **Delete on success** (only needed during the detector pass) |
| Staged `*.mp4` copies | WSL `~/clips` | ✅ from original | **Delete on success** |
| `*_wasbcache/` (resume cache) | WSL + `output/wasb` | ✅ | Keep only while a run is in-flight/iterating; drop once CSV exists |
| `chunks_*_handoff/`, `tmp*/` | Windows `output/` | ✅ | Auto-clean per run; sweep stragglers on crash |
| 1080p proxy `*_1080p.mp4` | Windows `output/` | ✅ from 4K | Keep while iterating on that video; purge when done |
| **Trajectory CSVs** | `~/clips`, `output/wasb` | ⚠️ GPU-only | **Keep**; archive to `Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories` |
| **SQLite DB (rallies)** | `output/` | ⚠️ Gemini cost | **Keep** — this is what makes re-stitch free |
| **Final reels** | `output/` | ⚠️ expensive | Keep until copied out, then purge |

## Tier 1 — Make the pipeline self-clean (the real fix)

The highest-leverage change: **delete the `*_frames/` dir and staged video automatically once the trajectory CSV is written successfully.** Frames are purely intermediate — after the CSV exists they're dead weight. Done right this caps per-video frame bloat to "during the run only," so it never accumulates again.

- Add `indexing.keep_frames: false` (default) to config; gate deletion on it so debugging can opt to retain.
- **Only delete on success** (CSV present) — on failure, keep frames + `wasbcache` so the resume-across-reboots design still works.
- Wire it in `wasb_runner.run_predict` / `tracknet_runner` (they already know the frames path). This is a clean, testable PR.

## Tier 2 — A dry-run-first cleanup tool

Replace ad-hoc `rm` with `tools/cleanup_caches.py` (or scratch first):
- Scans **both** WSL `~/clips` and Windows `output/{wasb,chunks_*_handoff,tmp*}`.
- Classifies each entry (regenerable vs durable) and prints a **sized report — dry-run by default** (exactly the review-first flow you just used).
- `--apply` to delete; `--keep-video <id>` to protect current work; `--older-than 7d` / `--min-size` filters.
- One command, repeatable, no per-session re-discovery of paths.

## Tier 3 — Cadence + guardrails (so it actually happens)

- **Disk preflight in the runner**: check `df` before extraction; warn (or refuse) if free space < threshold (e.g. 100 GB), optionally offering to GC the oldest frame caches.
- **Cadence tied to the existing checkpoint convention**: run the cleanup tool's dry-run at session wrap and after each completed video; that's when frames are safe to drop (CSV + DB rallies exist).
- **Optional passive hook**: a `Stop`/`SessionEnd` hook in `settings.json` that runs the dry-run *report only* (never auto-deletes) so you always see accumulation before it's a problem.

## Tier 4 — Guarantee outputs are durable first

Cleanup is only safe because outputs survive: ensure trajectory CSVs land in `Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories` and reels get copied out, *then* intermediates are always disposable. This is already the convention — Tier 1/2 just lean on it.

---

**My recommendation:** Tier 1 is the one that actually ends the problem (the pipeline stops hoarding 93 GB/video); Tier 2 makes the occasional manual sweep safe and fast. Tiers 3–4 are policy/cadence, mostly already in your conventions.

Want me to:

1	"""Abstract base for detector runners (TrackNet, WASB, future native, etc.).
2	
3	This completes the Detector Runner Abstraction (low-regret item 6 continuation / item 1
4	of the post-observability re-prioritization) to de-risk the WSL/conda dependency for
5	the high-accuracy shuttle trajectory substrate used by tracknet_hybrid / wasb_hybrid.
6	
7	The ABC lets us:
8	- Keep the current WSL adapters working unchanged for Windows users.
9	- Swap in (or A/B test) a Linux-native implementation later with zero changes to
10	  the hybrid segmenters or the model-agnostic windowing brain.
11	- Provide a single place to evolve the healthcheck / error contract and thread
12	  detector readiness into per-video reports.
13	
14	Contract (deliberately matches what the concrete runners + hybrids + tests already did):
15	- run_predict(video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str) -> Optional[str]
16	  Run detector, return the Windows-side path to the produced trajectory CSV
17	  (e.g. ".../clip_predict.csv" or ".../clip_wasb.csv"), or None on any failure.
18	  The caller (hybrid) is responsible for output dir; impls may stage the video
19	  into WSL fs for perf.
20	
21	- healthcheck() -> tuple[bool, str]
22	  Return (ok, message). Never raises. "ok" decides whether to proceed to run_predict.
23	  Message is logged (and can be surfaced to reports or UX in future).
24	  Intended to be cheap (env existence, import, GPU visibility, weights present).
25	
26	Existing behavior is 100% preserved. The only additions are the inheritance and
27	the explicit common type for future implementers.
28	
29	How to add a new runner (for docs / future contributors):
30	1. Subclass DetectorRunner in a new or existing module under pipeline/detectors/.
31	2. Implement run_predict and healthcheck (return (bool, str) tuple).
32	3. If it produces the same CSV schema (Frame,Visibility,X,Y), reuse or re-export
33	   TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv and .trajectory_to_action_windows (they are
34	   deliberately static and model-agnostic). If the new detector needs different
35	   windowing, it can implement its own or we generalize later.
36	4. Lazy-import heavy native deps inside the methods (so importing the segmenter
37	   registry never pulls torch/tf on a machine that doesn't have them).
38	5. Update the two hybrid segmenters (or introduce a detector= choice) to accept
39	   DetectorRunner instances (they already do the right thing via duck-typing + the
40	   type hints we add here).
41	6. Add unit tests that exercise the contract (isinstance, healthcheck shape, run
42	   returns str|None) using mocks for the external bits — see test_tracknet_runner.py.
43	7. No change to eval/calibration code paths (they consume the CSV + the static
44	   windowing helpers directly).
45	
46	Optional Linux-native path (the main de-risk goal):
47	A LinuxNativeRunner (or ONNXRunner) would:
48	- Skip all wsl / _stage / conda activate.
49	- Load weights locally (torch or onnx) or call a native binary.
50	- Write the exact same CSV format so trajectory_to_action_windows works verbatim.
51	- healthcheck can just check "weights file exists and torch importable + CUDA?".
52	This removes the WSL requirement for Linux users / future SaaS workers while
53	keeping the exact same candidate quality characteristics (or better, once native
54	weights are available).
55	
56	Error surfacing into reports:
57	The healthcheck message is already logged by the hybrids on failure. The reporting
58	harvest sees the outcome as "0 candidates" + detector name. When we wire richer
59	segmenter Results (or pass detector_health into emit_indexer_report context), the
60	(ok, msg) can be recorded explicitly under segmentation.details or a new detector
61	stage. The ABC makes that future change local to one place.
62	
63	See also:
64	- backend/pipeline/segmenters/{tracknet_hybrid,wasb_hybrid}.py (the two callers)
65	- tests/test_tracknet_runner.py (contract + pure logic tests; hybrids mock the runner)
66	- docs/TRACKNET_WSL_SETUP.md and CODE_MAP.md (2.2 Detectors section)
67	"""
68	
69	import logging
70	import shlex
71	import subprocess
72	from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
73	from typing import Any, Callable, Optional, Tuple
74	
75	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
76	
77	
78	def wsl_tilde_quote(path: str) -> str:
79	    """shlex.quote that still lets bash expand a leading ``~/``.
80	
81	    ``shlex.quote("~/clips/x.mp4")`` single-quotes the whole path, so bash
82	    treats ``~`` literally and ``cp``/``mkdir`` fail with "No such file or
83	    directory" — this broke live WSL staging for the default ``~/clips``
84	    stage dir (found by the 2026-06-11 wasb_hybrid smoke run; the BACKLOG
85	    "shlex-quote breaks ~ expansion" item). Quoting only the part after
86	    ``~/`` keeps expansion AND protects spaces/specials in the remainder.
87	    """
88	    if path == "~":
89	        return path
90	    if path.startswith("~/"):
91	        return "~/" + shlex.quote(path[2:])
92	    return shlex.quote(path)
93	
94	
95	class WslCommandMixin:
96	    """Shared ``wsl -d <distro> bash -c 'source <conda_sh> && <cmd>'`` invocation.
97	
98	    Lives beside (not on) DetectorRunner: a future Linux-native runner must not
99	    inherit WSL plumbing. Requires ``self.cfg`` with ``distro``, ``conda_sh``
100	    and ``timeout_sec`` attributes (TrackNetConfig and WasbConfig both qualify).
101	    ``timeout_sec == 0`` maps to None = wait forever (configs default 1800s).
102	    """
103	
104	    cfg: Any
105	
106	    def _wsl_bash(self, inner_cmd: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
107	        full = f"source {self.cfg.conda_sh} && {inner_cmd}"
108	        argv = ["wsl", "-d", self.cfg.distro, "bash", "-c", full]
109	        logger.debug("WSL exec: %s", full)
110	        return subprocess.run(argv, capture_output=True, text=True,
111	                              timeout=(self.cfg.timeout_sec or None))
112	
113	
114	class DetectorRunner(ABC):
115	    """Common interface for shuttle/ball trajectory detectors (WSL or otherwise)."""
116	
117	    @abstractmethod
118	    def run_predict(self, video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str,
119	                    video_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:
120	        """Run inference and return path to trajectory CSV (or None on failure).
121	
122	        ``video_id`` (the file MD5) namespaces all staged/cached/output artifacts so two
123	        videos that happen to share a filename cannot reuse each other's frames or CSV.
124	        It is optional for back-compat; callers in the live pipeline always pass it.
125	        """
126	        pass
127	
128	    @abstractmethod
129	    def healthcheck(self) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
130	        """Quick check if the runner (env, weights, etc.) is usable.
131	
132	        Returns (ok, message). Never raises. Cheap to call before a long run.
133	        """
134	        pass
135	
136	    # De facto trajectory contract: the trajectory hybrids call these
137	    # unconditionally after run_predict. Concrete runners reuse/re-export the
138	    # model-agnostic TrackNetRunner staticmethods (same CSV schema + windowing);
139	    # a runner with custom windowing supplies its own with the same shapes.
140	    parse_trajectory_csv: Callable[..., Any]
141	    trajectory_to_action_windows: Callable[..., Any]
142

64	class IndexingConfig(BaseModel):
65	    default_segmenter: str = "yolo_hybrid"
66	    use_gpu: bool = True
67	    motion_threshold: float = 0.08
68	    min_segment_duration: float = 3.0
69	    dead_time_merge_gap: float = 2.0
70	    chunk_duration: float = 90.0
71	    chunk_overlap: float = 10.0
72	    detector_model: str = "fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn"
73	    detector_score_threshold: float = 0.5
74	    yolo_velocity_threshold: float = 0.15
75	    yolo_frame_skip: int = 3
76	    yolo_tracker: str = "bytetrack.yaml"
77	    yolo_prefetch_workers: int = 2
78	    min_action_window_duration: float = 2.0
79	    log_yolo_candidates: bool = True
80	    store_yolo_candidates: bool = True
81	    ai_handoff_padding: float = 2.0
82	    ai_max_workers: int = 5
83	    # Width (px) the gemini segmenter re-encodes chunks to before upload. Stream-copied
84	    # chunks of a high-bitrate (4K) source blow past the provider upload cap
85	    # (backend/providers/gemini.py::MAX_UPLOAD_MB) and are refused — observed live as
86	    # "0 segments / success". Re-encoding also makes chunk boundaries frame-accurate
87	    # (stream copy cuts at the nearest keyframe). 0 = legacy stream-copy at source res.
88	    # Matches gemini_refine's --clip-scale-width default.
89	    ai_chunk_scale_width: int = 1280
90	    # Optional debug knob: trim every video to the first N seconds before processing.
91	    debug_duration: Optional[float] = None
92	
93	    tracknet: TrackNetConfig = Field(default_factory=TrackNetConfig)
94	    wasb: WasbIndexConfig = Field(default_factory=WasbIndexConfig)
95	
96	    @field_validator("default_segmenter")
97	    @classmethod
98	    def segmenter_must_be_registered(cls, v: str) -> str:
99	        # Registry check happens at runtime in main/segmenter selection.
100	        # Here we just allow any string for forward compatibility.
101	        return v
102	
103	    @model_validator(mode="after")
104	    def _chunk_step_must_be_positive(self) -> "IndexingConfig":
105	        # The chunked segmenters advance by (chunk_duration - chunk_overlap); a non-positive
106	        # step makes `while t < duration: t += step` loop forever, growing memory before any
107	        # GPU work. Reject the bad config at load time instead.
108	        if self.chunk_overlap >= self.chunk_duration:
109	            raise ValueError(
110	                f"indexing.chunk_overlap ({self.chunk_overlap}) must be < chunk_duration "
111	                f"({self.chunk_duration}); otherwise chunking never advances."
112	            )
113	        return self
114

### 448. assistant (2026-06-13T15:19:00.447Z)

"""Cache-hygiene tool for the GPU highlight pipeline.

Scans the regenerable intermediates the pipeline leaves behind — in the WSL stage
dir (``~/clips``) and the Windows ``output/`` tree — classifies each entry as
**purge** (regenerable: frame caches, staged video copies, handoff clips, temp dirs)
or **keep** (durable: trajectory CSVs, the SQLite DB, final reels) and reports the
reclaimable space.

DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT — it only *shows* what it would delete. Pass ``--apply`` to delete.

    python -m tools.cleanup_caches                      # report only (safe)
    python -m tools.cleanup_caches --apply              # delete purge candidates
    python -m tools.cleanup_caches --keep-video 89605e80ed01   # protect one video's cache
    python -m tools.cleanup_caches --older-than 7       # only purge entries >7 days old
    python -m tools.cleanup_caches --include-wasbcache  # also drop detector resume caches

Pairs with the pipeline's own self-cleaning (indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.keep_frames=false,
which deletes frames after a SUCCESSFUL run); this tool reclaims what failed/old runs
and pre-self-cleaning videos left behind. Stdlib only; safe to run anywhere (the WSL
scan is skipped automatically when ``wsl`` is unavailable).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List, Optional, Tuple

# ---- classification (pure; unit-tested) ------------------------------------- #

# (category, regenerable, default_purge). default_purge=True → deleted unless a flag
# protects it. wasbcache is regenerable but default_purge=False (it enables resume and
# is tiny) — opt in with --include-wasbcache.
_KEEP = ("keep", False, False)


def classify(name: str, is_dir: bool, location: str) -> Tuple[str, bool, bool]:
    """Classify a cache entry by name. Returns (category, regenerable, default_purge)."""
    low = name.lower()
    if is_dir:
        if low.endswith("_frames"):
            return ("frame cache", True, True)
        if low.endswith("_wasbcache"):
            return ("detector resume cache", True, False)  # opt-in via --include-wasbcache
        if low.startswith("chunks_") and low.endswith("_handoff"):
            return ("ai handoff clips", True, True)
        if low.startswith("tmp"):
            return ("temp dir", True, True)
        return ("other dir", False, False)
    # files
    if low.endswith((".csv",)):
        return ("trajectory csv", False, False)          # durable analysis output
    if low.endswith((".db", ".sqlite", ".sqlite3")):
        return ("database", False, False)                # cached rallies — never purge
    if low.endswith((".log", ".sh", ".txt", ".json", ".md")):
        return ("log/script", False, False)
    if low.endswith((".mp4", ".avi", ".mov", ".mkv")):
        # In the WSL stage dir, every video is a regenerable staged copy.
        if location == "wsl":
            return ("staged video", True, True)
        # In output/, a 1080p/proxy is regenerable; anything else is a produced reel.
        if "_1080p" in low or "_proxy" in low or low.endswith("_proxy.mp4"):
            return ("proxy video", True, True)
        return ("highlight reel", False, False)          # durable output — keep
    return ("other", False, False)


@dataclass
class Entry:
    name: str
    path: str            # display path (WSL ~/clips/<name> or windows abs)
    size: int            # bytes
    mtime: float         # epoch seconds (0 if unknown)
    is_dir: bool
    location: str        # "wsl" | "windows"

    @property
    def category(self) -> Tuple[str, bool, bool]:
        return classify(self.name, self.is_dir, self.location)


def plan_deletions(entries: List[Entry], *, keep_video: Optional[str], older_than_days: float,
                   include_wasbcache: bool, now: float) -> Tuple[List[Entry], List[Entry]]:
    """Split entries into (to_delete, kept) per the flags. Pure — no I/O."""
    to_delete: List[Entry] = []
    kept: List[Entry] = []
    cutoff = older_than_days * 86400.0
    for e in entries:
        cat, _regen, default_purge = e.category
        purge = default_purge or (include_wasbcache and cat == "detector resume cache")
        if purge and keep_video and keep_video in e.name:
            purge = False
        if purge and older_than_days > 0 and e.mtime > 0 and (now - e.mtime) < cutoff:
            purge = False
        (to_delete if purge else kept).append(e)
    return to_delete, kept


# ---- scanning (I/O) --------------------------------------------------------- #

def human(n: int) -> str:
    f = float(n)
    for unit in ("B", "K", "M", "G", "T"):
        if f < 1024 or unit == "T":
            return f"{f:.1f}{unit}" if unit != "B" else f"{int(f)}B"
        f /= 1024
    return f"{f:.1f}T"


def scan_wsl(distro: str, stage_dir: str) -> List[Entry]:
    """List ~/clips entries with size+mtime via one `wsl` call. [] if WSL absent."""
    if not shutil.which("wsl"):
        return []
    script = (
        f'cd {stage_dir} 2>/dev/null || exit 0; '
        'for e in * .[!.]*; do [ -e "$e" ] || continue; '
        'd=0; [ -d "$e" ] && d=1; '
        'sz=$(du -sb "$e" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1); '
        'mt=$(stat -c %Y "$e" 2>/dev/null); '
        r'printf "%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n" "$d" "$sz" "$mt" "$e"; done'
    )
    try:
        res = subprocess.run(["wsl", "-d", distro, "bash", "-lc", script],
                             capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=300)
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
        return []
    entries: List[Entry] = []
    for line in (res.stdout or "").splitlines():
        parts = line.split("\t")
        if len(parts) != 4:
            continue
        d, sz, mt, name = parts
        try:
            entries.append(Entry(name=name, path=f"{stage_dir}/{name}",
                                 size=int(sz or 0), mtime=float(mt or 0),
                                 is_dir=(d == "1"), location="wsl"))
        except ValueError:
            continue
    return entries


def _dir_size(path: str) -> int:
    total = 0
    for root, _dirs, files in os.walk(path):
        for fn in files:
            try:
                total += os.path.getsize(os.path.join(root, fn))
            except OSError:
                pass
    return total


def scan_windows(output_dir: str) -> List[Entry]:
    """List output/ entries (top level) plus the output/wasb sub-tree."""
    entries: List[Entry] = []
    for base in (output_dir, os.path.join(output_dir, "wasb")):
        if not os.path.isdir(base):
            continue
        for de in os.scandir(base):
            try:
                is_dir = de.is_dir()
                size = _dir_size(de.path) if is_dir else de.stat().st_size
                mtime = de.stat().st_mtime
            except OSError:
                continue
            entries.append(Entry(name=de.name, path=de.path, size=size,
                                 mtime=mtime, is_dir=is_dir, location="windows"))
    return entries


def _delete(e: Entry, distro: str) -> bool:
    try:
        if e.location == "wsl":
            subprocess.run(["wsl", "-d", distro, "bash", "-lc", f'rm -rf {_q(e.path)}'],
                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=600, check=True)
        elif e.is_dir:
            shutil.rmtree(e.path, ignore_errors=True)
        else:
            os.remove(e.path)
        return True
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        return False


def _q(path: str) -> str:
    if path.startswith("~/"):
        return "~/'" + path[2:].replace("'", "'\\''") + "'"
    return "'" + path.replace("'", "'\\''") + "'"


def _report(title: str, rows: List[Entry]) -> int:
    print(f"\n{title}")
    if not rows:
        print("  (none)")
        return 0
    total = 0
    for e in sorted(rows, key=lambda x: x.size, reverse=True):
        cat = e.category[0]
        print(f"  {human(e.size):>8}  {cat:<22} {e.path}")
        total += e.size
    print(f"  {'-' * 8}")
    print(f"  {human(total):>8}  TOTAL ({len(rows)} items)")
    return total


def main(argv: Optional[List[str]] = None) -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Report/clean regenerable pipeline caches (dry-run by default).")
    ap.add_argument("--apply", action="store_true", help="actually delete (default: report only)")
    ap.add_argument("--keep-video", default=None, help="protect entries whose name contains this id/substring")
    ap.add_argument("--older-than", type=float, default=0.0, help="only purge entries older than N days")
    ap.add_argument("--include-wasbcache", action="store_true", help="also purge detector resume caches")
    ap.add_argument("--stage-dir", default="~/clips", help="WSL stage dir (default ~/clips)")
    ap.add_argument("--distro", default="Ubuntu", help="WSL distro (default Ubuntu)")
    ap.add_argument("--output-dir", default="output", help="Windows output dir (default output)")
    args = ap.parse_args(argv)

    import time
    now = time.time()
    entries = scan_wsl(args.distro, args.stage_dir) + scan_windows(args.output_dir)
    to_delete, kept = plan_deletions(
        entries, keep_video=args.keep_video, older_than_days=args.older_than,
        include_wasbcache=args.include_wasbcache, now=now)

    reclaim = _report("PURGE candidates (regenerable):", to_delete)
    _report("KEEP (durable / protected):", kept)
    print(f"\nReclaimable: {human(reclaim)}")

    if not args.apply:
        print("\nDRY RUN — nothing deleted. Re-run with --apply to delete the PURGE list.")
        return 0
    if not to_delete:
        print("\nNothing to delete.")
        return 0

    print(f"\nDeleting {len(to_delete)} items...")
    freed, failed = 0, 0
    for e in to_delete:
        if _delete(e, args.distro):
            freed += e.size
        else:
            failed += 1
            print(f"  FAILED: {e.path}")
    print(f"Freed ~{human(freed)}" + (f" ({failed} failed)" if failed else ""))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

def run(label, script):
    r = subprocess.run(["wsl", "-d", "Ubuntu", "bash", "-lc", script],
                       capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)
    print(f"=== {label} === rc={r.returncode}")
    print("STDOUT:", repr(r.stdout[:300]))
    print("STDERR:", repr(r.stderr[:200]))
    print()

run("B: cd guarded + loop echo", 'cd ~/clips 2>/dev/null || exit 0; for e in *; do echo "X$e"; done | head -3')
run("C: full scan loop", (
    'cd ~/clips 2>/dev/null || exit 0; '

    'mt=$(stat -c %Y "$e" 2>/dev/null); '

### 481. assistant (2026-06-13T15:26:22.852Z)

"""Unit tests for the cache-hygiene tool's pure logic (classify + plan_deletions).

The I/O (wsl/du/find scan, deletion) is not unit-tested here — these guard the
classification + filtering rules that decide what gets deleted, which is the part
where a mistake would be destructive.
"""
from tools.cleanup_caches import Entry, classify, plan_deletions


def test_classify_regenerable_intermediates():
    assert classify("GX010125_1080p__abc_frames", True, "wsl")[2] is True       # frame cache -> purge
    assert classify("GX010125_1080p__abc.mp4", False, "wsl")[2] is True          # staged video -> purge
    assert classify("GX010125_1080p.mp4", False, "windows")[2] is True           # proxy -> purge
    assert classify("chunks_gemini_handoff", True, "windows")[2] is True         # handoff clips -> purge
    assert classify("tmp9xk2", True, "windows")[2] is True                       # temp dir -> purge


def test_classify_durable_kept():
    assert classify("adarsh_traj.csv", False, "wsl")[2] is False                 # trajectory -> keep
    assert classify("sports_indexer.db", False, "windows")[2] is False           # DB -> keep
    assert classify("GX010125_highlights.mp4", False, "windows")[2] is False     # reel -> keep
    assert classify("run_wasb.sh", False, "wsl")[2] is False                     # script -> keep
    # wasbcache is regenerable but opt-in only (default_purge False)
    cat, regen, default_purge = classify("adarsh_traj_wasbcache", True, "wsl")
    assert cat == "detector resume cache" and regen is True and default_purge is False


def _e(name, size, is_dir, location="wsl", mtime=0.0):
    return Entry(name=name, path=f"~/clips/{name}", size=size, mtime=mtime,
                 is_dir=is_dir, location=location)


def test_plan_default_purges_frames_keeps_csv_and_wasbcache():
    entries = [
        _e("v_frames", 90_000, True),
        _e("v.mp4", 2_000, False),
        _e("v_traj.csv", 100, False),
        _e("v_traj_wasbcache", 500, True),
    ]
    to_del, kept = plan_deletions(entries, keep_video=None, older_than_days=0,
                                  include_wasbcache=False, now=1_000_000.0)
    names = {e.name for e in to_del}
    assert names == {"v_frames", "v.mp4"}
    assert {e.name for e in kept} == {"v_traj.csv", "v_traj_wasbcache"}


def test_plan_include_wasbcache_opts_it_in():
    entries = [_e("v_traj_wasbcache", 500, True)]
    to_del, _ = plan_deletions(entries, keep_video=None, older_than_days=0,
                               include_wasbcache=True, now=1_000_000.0)
    assert [e.name for e in to_del] == ["v_traj_wasbcache"]


def test_plan_keep_video_protects_matching_substring():
    entries = [_e("GX010125_frames", 90_000, True), _e("kushagra_frames", 5_000, True)]
    to_del, kept = plan_deletions(entries, keep_video="GX010125", older_than_days=0,
                                  include_wasbcache=False, now=1_000_000.0)
    assert [e.name for e in to_del] == ["kushagra_frames"]
    assert [e.name for e in kept] == ["GX010125_frames"]


def test_plan_older_than_protects_recent():
    now = 1_000_000.0
    entries = [
        _e("recent_frames", 90_000, True, mtime=now - 3600),        # 1h old
        _e("old_frames", 90_000, True, mtime=now - 10 * 86400),     # 10d old
    ]
    to_del, kept = plan_deletions(entries, keep_video=None, older_than_days=7,
                                  include_wasbcache=False, now=now)
    assert [e.name for e in to_del] == ["old_frames"]
    assert [e.name for e in kept] == ["recent_frames"]
